\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\left\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{\prime}-M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\right\},

(S12)

since
\nabla_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}=\mathbf{0}
by definition. Evaluating (
S12
) at
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{\prime}=\tilde{M}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})
and substituting the resulting expression into (
S9
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\xi}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})

\displaystyle\approx\nabla^{2}_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\left\{\tilde{M}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})-M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\right\}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\delta}$}}}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}),

where
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\delta}$}}}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\equiv\tilde{M}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})-M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})
is the approximation error that appears in (
S6
).
Now, as
m\rightarrow\infty
, assuming the law of large numbers holds, and suitable regularity conditions on
\log p({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2})
, we have that
M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\to M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})
, and

-\nabla^{2}_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\longrightarrow-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\left[\nabla^{2}_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}\log p(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\right]\equiv{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}).

(S13)

Therefore, for large
m
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\xi}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\}

\displaystyle\approx\mathbb{E}\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\delta}$}}}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\}\approx-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}$}}}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}),

(S14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\xi}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\}

\displaystyle\approx\mathbb{V}\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\delta}$}}}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}).

(S15)

We now derive the asymptotic expectation and asymptotic variance of the innovations
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})
defined in (
S11
). Suppose it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}^{(j)}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\}_{j=1}^{m}}\left[\nabla_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}\mathbb{E}_{\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}^{(j)}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\}_{j=1}^{m}}\left[Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\right].

Then,

\mathbb{E}_{\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}^{(j)}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\}_{j=1}^{m}}\left[\nabla_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\right]=\nabla_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scri (S16)

since the gradient at the true maximizer is zero by definition. Therefore, for large

,

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}^{(j)}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\}_{j=1}^{m}}\left[\nabla_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}Q_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}_{\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}^{(j)}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\}_{j=1}^{m}}\left[\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\nabla_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}}\log p(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}^{(j)})\big|_{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}=M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})}\right]

we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\}

\displaystyle\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})^{-1}\mathbb{V}\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\xi}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\xi}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})^{-1}+\frac{1}{m}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})+\frac{1}{m}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})^{-1}.

Having obtained the first-order autoregressive formulation (
S10
) and a formal derivation of its asymptotic properties, we now consider the behavior near an isolated local maximizer
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
of the incomplete-data posterior density
p({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1})
. Since
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
is an isolated local maximizer of
p({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1})
, it is also an asymptotically stable fixed point of
M(\cdot)

(
Chan and Ledolter,, 1995
, Lemma 1)
. In particular,
M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
, and hence a first-order Taylor expansion of
M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})
around
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
yields:

\displaystyle M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})

\displaystyle\approx M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\left({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\left({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}\right),

(S17)

where
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\equiv\nabla_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}M({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})\big|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}}
. Substituting the approximation (
S17
) into (
S10
) yields

\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l)}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\Big\{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l-1)}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}\Big\}+\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{m}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l-1)}),

(S18)

and therefore
\{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l)}\}
is approximately an AR(1) process with autoregressive parameter
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})
. Further, substituting the approximation (
S17
) into (
S4
) yields the linearized system (i.e., linear approximation to (
S4
)),

{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{(l)}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\Big\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{(l-1)}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}\Big\}.

(S19)

Under the assumption that the local maximizer
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
is also an asymptotically stable fixed point of (
S19
), the spectral radius of
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})
is strictly less than one
(
Elaydi,, 2005
, Thm. 4.13)
. Therefore, the AR(1) process (
S18
) that approximates
\{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l)}\}
is stationary, and the innovations can be treated as independent and identically distributed with mean and variance evaluated at
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
. This gives the following stationary AR(1) representation:

\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l)}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\Big\{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l-1)}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}\Big\}+\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}),

(S20)

where
\mathbb{E}\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\epsilon}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\epsilon}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\epsilon}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\epsilon}$}}}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})
and
\mathbb{V}\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\epsilon}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\epsilon}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\epsilon}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\epsilon}$}}}_{m}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})+\frac{1}{m}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})^{-1}
. The process (
S20
) has mean

\mathbb{E}\{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l)}\}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}+\{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{I}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{I}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{I}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{I}$}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})\}^{-1}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}),

(S21)

where
\textstyle{I}
denotes the identity matrix; and variance
\mathbb{V}\{\tilde{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}_{m}^{(l)}\}\approx{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{C}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})
, where
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{C}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})
is the solution to
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{C}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{C}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{C}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}){\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})^{\!\scriptscriptstyle\top}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})+\frac{1}{m}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*})^{-1}
.

Importantly, in the ideal special case of mean-zero approximation error,
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}})={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{0}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{0}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{0}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{0}$}}}
, and the mean
(
S21
) reduces to
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
, allowing one to recover
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}^{*}
through the averaging procedure described in Section
2.3.1
of the main text.

S3
Continuous approximations of the 0–1 loss function

In Section
2.3.3
of the main text, we describe how an NBE can be constructed to approximate the MAP estimator. The approach hinges on the use of a continuous approximation of the 0–1 loss function. In this section, we consider several candidate loss functions.

In the main text, we adopt the loss function,

L_{\rm{TANH}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}};\kappa)=\rm{tanh}(\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa),\quad\kappa>0.

(S22)

where
\|\cdot\|
denotes the Euclidean norm (although any norm in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
could be used) and
d
denotes the dimension of
\textstyle{\theta}
. For
d=1
, Figure
S1
, panel A, shows (
S22
) and its gradient for
\kappa\in\{1,0.5,0.1,0.05\}
. For fixed
\kappa
, the gradient of (
S22
) is bounded as
\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|\to 0
, and it yields the 0–1 loss function in the limit as
\kappa\to 0
, which we now prove.

Proof.

From the definition of the hyperbolic tangent function, it follows that

\displaystyle L_{\rm{TANH}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}};\kappa)

\displaystyle=\rm{tanh}(\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp(\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)-\exp(-\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)}{\exp(\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)+\exp(-\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)},

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\exp(-2\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)}{1+\exp(-2\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)}.

First, suppose that
\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}
, so that
\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|=0
. In this case, we have that

L_TANH(
\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}
,
^

\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}

;
κ
)|_
^

\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}

=
\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}

=

1 - exp(0)

1 + exp(0)

= 0.

Second, suppose that
\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}
, so that
\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|>0
. In this case, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\kappa\to 0}L_{\rm{TANH}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}};\kappa)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\kappa\to 0}\frac{1-\exp(-2\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)}{1+\exp(-2\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\kappa)}=1,

since
\lim_{x\to\infty}e^{-ax}=0
for all
a>0
. Therefore, we obtain

\lim_{\kappa\to 0}L_{\rm{TANH}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}};\kappa)=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\\
1&{\text{otherwise}},\end{cases}

which is the 0–1 loss function.
∎

Figure S1
:
(A) The loss function (
S22
) and its gradient for
\kappa\in\{1,0.5,0.1,0.05\}
. (B) The loss function (
S24
) and its gradient for
\beta\in\{1,0.5,0.1,0.05\}
and
\delta\in\{0,0.1\}
. For
\delta=0
and
\beta<1
, the gradient diverges at the origin; for
\delta=0.1
, the gradient at the origin is finite and decreases as
\beta
decreases.

Other continuous approximations are available. For example, the loss function

L({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}};\beta)=\big(\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|)^{\beta},\quad\beta>0,

(S23)

generalizes the loss function given in
Cressie, (2022, Eqn. 6)
to the multiparameter setting, and it also yields the 0–1 loss in the limit as
\beta\to 0
. A possible surrogate for the 0–1 loss function is therefore given by (
S23
) with
\beta
close to zero. However, with
\beta<1
, the gradient of (
S23
) diverges as
\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|\to 0
, which can cause numerical instability during training. There are several ways to alleviate this issue. For instance, one may add a small positive constant
\delta
to
\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|
in (
S23
), yielding the loss function,

L_{\rm{POW}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}};\beta,\delta)=\big(\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|+\delta)^{\beta}-\delta^{\beta},\quad\beta>0,\delta>0,

(S24)

Figure
S1
, panel B, shows (
S24
) and its gradient for
\beta\in\{1,0.5,0.1,0.05\}
and
\delta\in\{0,0.1\}
.

Another possible surrogate loss function is given by

L_{\rm{CORR}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}},\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}};\rho,\alpha)=1-\{1+(\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|/\rho)^{\alpha}\}^{-1},\quad\rho>0,\,\alpha\geq 1,

(S25)

which yields the 0–1 loss in the limit as
\rho\to 0
, where the right-hand side of (
S25
) is a variogram constructed from the Cauchy correlation function
(
Gneiting and Schlather,, 2004
)
. Similar to (
S24
) and (
S22
), the gradient of (
S25
) is bounded. In general, similarly constructed variogram models provide a broad family of functions that may serve as continuous approximations of the 0–1 loss function, with various degrees of differentiability at the origin that may be controlled by the practitioner. These classes are mentioned here for completeness; in this paper we use the loss function (
S22
), which we find to work well in practice.

Finally, we make practical remarks relating to the training of NBEs under continuous approximations of the 0–1 loss function.
First, for most conventional norms,
\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|
tends to increase as the dimension
d
of
\textstyle{\theta}
increases. This represents a potential pitfall since, for most approximations of the 0–1 loss function, like (
S22
), the gradient vanishes as
\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\|
becomes large. A simple remedy for this is to replace the Euclidean norm with a scaled norm, for instance,
\|\cdot\|_{p}/d^{1/p}
, where
\|\cdot\|_{p}
denotes the
L_{p}
norm, to reduce the growth of distances with increasing dimension.
Second, to prevent the NBE from becoming “stuck” in regions of the parameter space where gradients are extremely small at the start of training, it can be beneficial to pretrain the network using conventional, well behaved loss functions, such as the mean-absolute-error or the mean-squared-error loss functions. Importantly, once the NBE has been pretrained, it can be trained under an approximation of the 0–1 loss function at almost no additional computational cost. This is achieved by fixing the initial portion of the network (often referred to as the “summary network”, when the architecture is viewed in a modular fashion), which typically contains relatively expensive operations, such as convolution. Then updating occurs only in the latter portion of the network (often referred to as the “inference network”), which typically consists of a simple, fast-to-evaluate multilayer perceptron. In our experiments, the pretraining stage using the mean-absolute-error loss function, ranges from several minutes to a few hours, whereas training under the approximate 0–1 loss function requires only a matter of seconds.

S4
Additional simulation study

In Section
3
of the main text, we conducted simulation studies involving spatial versions of the Gaussian process model and the hidden Potts model. Here, we conduct an additional simulation experiment, using a spatial version of a popular normal mean-variance mixture known as the generalized hyperbolic (GH) distribution. The likelihood is available for this case, permitting comparison with a gold-standard likelihood-based estimator, namely the MAP estimator obtained by directly maximizing the posterior density. Here, we also consider a conventional simulation-based approach, approximate Bayesian computation
(
Tavaré et al.,, 1997
;
Pritchard et al.,, 1999
;
Lintusaari et al.,, 2017
;
Sisson et al.,, 2018
, ABC;)
. Before proceeding with the analysis, we first review the GH distribution, including its density function and the formulas required for conditional simulation.

The
n
-dimensional random vector
\textstyle{Z}
is called a NMVM if it can be represented as,

{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}+M{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}+\sqrt{M}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}},

(S26)

where
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
M
is a positive latent random variable that is independent of the small-scale variation
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{V}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{V}$}}}\sim{\text{Gau}}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{0}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{0}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{0}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{0}$}}},{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}})
, and where
\textstyle{\Sigma}
is a covariance matrix. This family of distributions is closed under conditioning: for any partitioning of
\textstyle{Z}
into components
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}
and
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}
, and with
\textstyle{\mu}
,
\textstyle{\alpha}
, and
\textstyle{\Sigma}
partitioned accordingly,
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{2}\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}
is also a NMVM, with parameters

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}_{2\mid 1}

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}_{2}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{21}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{11}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}_{1}),

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}_{2\mid 1}

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}_{2}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{21}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{11}^{-1}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}_{1},

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{22\mid 1}

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{22}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{21}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{11}^{-1}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{12},

and with latent variable
M_{2|1}
that is distributed according to
M\mid{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}

(
Jamalizadeh and Balakrishnan,, 2019
, Theorem 1)
.

The generalized hyperbolic (GH) distribution is obtained when
M
in (
S26
) follows a generalized inverse Gaussian (GIG) distribution. The GH distribution is prominent in financial modeling due to its flexible marginal distributions and infinite divisibility
(
Barndorff-Nielsen,, 1997
;
Prause,, 1999
)
.
We consider the parameterization of the GIG density employed by
Browne and McNicholas, (2015)
,

f_{\rm{GIG}}(m;\omega,\phi,\lambda)=\frac{(m/\phi)^{\lambda-1}}{2\phi K_{\lambda}(\omega)}\exp{\bigg\{-\frac{\omega}{2}\bigg(\frac{\phi}{m}+\frac{m}{\phi}\bigg)\bigg\}},\quad m>0,

(S27)

with concentration parameter
\omega>0
, shape parameter
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, scale parameter
\phi>0
, and where
K_{\lambda}(\cdot)
denotes the modified Bessel function of the second kind of order
\lambda
. Then,
M_{2|1}
also follows a GIG distribution with parameters

\displaystyle\omega_{2\mid 1}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\{\omega\phi+({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}_{1})^{\prime}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{11}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}_{1})\}\{\omega\phi^{-1}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}_{1}^{\prime}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{11}^{-1}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}_{1}\}},

\displaystyle\phi_{2\mid 1}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\{\omega\phi+({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}_{1})^{\prime}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{11}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\mu}$}}}_{1})\}/\{\omega\phi^{-1}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}_{1}^{\prime}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\Sigma}$}}}_{11}^{-1}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}_{1}\}},

\displaystyle\lambda_{2\mid 1}

\displaystyle=\lambda-n_{1}/2,

where
n_{1}

{\mathchoice{\hbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{Z}$}}{\hbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}}}_{1}

(
Jamalizadeh and Balakrishnan,, 2019

. Throughout, we fix
\phi=1
for identifiability reasons. We then write

, which has density function,
